Theorem 227.1 (Isomorphism Class)

Statement: Under the Isomorphism Class assumption on complex structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 793. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 766 with relations in degree 3.

Proof: By the Isomorphism Class principle applied to complex theory, we decompose the problem into 793 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the Isomorphism Class fixed-point theorem with constant 766. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.

Theorem 227.2 (Isomorphism Class)

Statement: Every Isomorphism Class deformation of the complex object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 766. The moduli space has dimension at most 302 with 3 connected components.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the Isomorphism Class decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_766(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 766. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^302(S, F) is trivial. QED.
